                                         SATELLITE PREDICTION SERVICE

                    1.        Reference is made to your letter SA5644/2/1
                    of 15th December 1966 to Department of Air, regarding the
                    offer to supply weekly satellite prediction ~ables.
                    2.        Although, at the time, the Department of Air
                    assessed that they had no requirement for these tables and
                    declined the offer, subsequent events indicate that there
                    is a need for such information at Corrnnand level.
                    3.        Would you, therefore, forward two copies of this
                    publication, on a continuing basis, to this Command marked
                    "For Attention C Intell 0".



                                                                   (E. K. KILDEY)
                                                                  Wing Commander
                                                             For Air Officer Commanding

                                   In reply to your query on folio 32 I should advise that there
                       is no proposal for PO/Ranges to devise a new form for the reporting of
                       "sightings" on ·!;he Woomera Range . Superintendent/Trials , in a, recent
                       memorandum to PO/Ranges on this s1tbjeot , merely requested t11at , if such
                       sightings should be reported by any members of the Range staff , the faot
                       should be notified thro1lgh the correct channel to this office .

                       2.                 I have since discussed the matter with tl1e Chief Security
                       Officer, Melbourne and the Regional Security Officer, Adelaide and they
                       have a.greed wi·th my suggestion that any report ori gi nating within the
                       \Voornera Range area should come ·t hrough this o.f.~<(,P..,•.to the Security Branch :Jlt
                       rather than going direct. This will Ila.Ve the ~advantage that -
                                  (a)     Such repo:-ets \VOuld be checked by senior. staff
                                          in Trials Wing to ensure that the level of
                                          reporting was acceptable .
                                  (b)     Only reports of "sightings" which could not be
                                          otherwise satisfactorily expla.:iined, \V011ld be for-
                                          warded to Security and thence to the Department ~f
                                          Air.
                                  (c)     This Establishment wo11ld be informed of such
                                          "sightings" before and not after they l1ad been
                                          notified to Head Office .

                       3.              I have noted that the activitief:l of the Woomera Cl llb have
                       decli ne(l rna.rkedJ..f it1 :recent months but I nevertheless thought it
                       desirable to propose the above arrangement in order to ensure that
                       any report emtnating from the Range would be such as not to bring
                       discredit on this EstablisllIDent.




                                                                            DEPUTY DIRECTOR/TRIALS

                 Officer in Charge,
                 SPECIAL BRANCH.

                 SUBJECT:   Report of unidentified object falling from outer-space.

                       I have to report that on 20/?/67 Senior Constable Seebohm
                 stationed at Karoonda reported to Operations Room, Police Head-
                 quarters, Adelaide that he had sighted an unidentified object
                 falling to earth near Karoonda on that date.    Special Branch was
                 notified and it as suggested that Senior Constable Seebohm submit
                 a report re the matter to that Branch.
                      On July 24th. 196?, a report submitted by Senior Constable
                 Seebohm was received at this Branch, quote z-
                      " I ish to report that at about 153? hours on Thursday the
                 20th of July 1 196? I sa an unidentified object fall to earth.
                        At the time my position was about one mile west ot Karoonda,
                 travelling ast along the main urray Bridge-Karoonda road.
                        The object appeared high in the sky almost due east of
                 Karoonda and travelled in a southerly direction to the earth at
                 an angle ot about forty five degrees.     y vie or the object
                 vas obscured by trees growing on the southern side of the road
                 during the final stage or its descent and I am unable to say if
                 it actually hit the earth.
                        The object had a silvery look and appeared to be about the
                 size of a football or slightly smaller, there was a bluish white
                 trail extending behind it for what appeared to the eye to be about
                 eight or ten feet.    The object itself had the appearance of the
                 sun shining on polished metal.    It was travelling at a very high
                 speed and was plainly visible.
                        The weather "as clear, bright sun light with a moderate
                 northerly wind.
                        From the clearness of the object I reel that it possibly
                 came to earth near Karoonda and a possible area would be from
                 five to ten miles south east.
                        Christopher DALL- ARD, Driver or Karoond& was a passenger

                          "UNIDENTI1i'IED Ii'LYING OBJECTStt BLUB.




                          In reply to your memorandum of the 25th July, 1967, the
             following report details the situation regarding an organisation existing
             at Woomera known as the "Scientific, Technical and Astronomical Research
             Society" henceforth called the STARS.

             2.          On receipt of your letter A 766/1/12 of 11th May, 1966, the
             Superintendent Woomera informed Mrs. P.J. Ross, who ultimately became
             the Secretary of the STARS ,

                         " (a) There would be no restriction placed on any t,Toup
                               of Woomera. personnel getting together to foster their
                               common interests.

                           (b) However you should be advised that as Woomera is in a
                               prohibited area, the disclosure of axiy information
                               originating in the Area ie prohibited.

                           (c) Under these circumstances then, you would not be per-
                               mitted to publish any such information either locally
                               or through any affiliated society such as the Victorian
                               Flying Saucer Research Society .

                          (d) Finally, should the existence of a club in Woomera be
                              publicised you would no doubt be inundated ancl
                              embarrassed by requests from other societies for
                              information. I could therefore not pennit any
                              publicity on the formation of such a club in Woomera.
                              to be made."

             3.          The Society was duly formed and conducted under the above
             conditions and a copy of the minutes of each meeting is sent to the
             Superintendent Woomera. These mir1utes are sent to the Establishment
             Security Officer for perusal .

             4.           About l•Tay this yea:r I was shown some "sightinff reports" which
             were rendered by members of STARS whilst engaged in their official duties.
             On ir1quiry I found that these forzns were designed by the STARS organisation
             so that members could be info1·1ned of occur1·encea within the Area. It was
             intended that these reports would be freely available to all members of the
             Society, but in view of the passibility of leakage of information I
             instructed the E.S.O. that the reports then held were not bo be released,
             and that the reporting system was to be discontinued.




                                                                                     /2

National Archives of Australia                                        NAA: 0174, SA5644/2/1
                                                                         COPY ONLY

                                            2.

             5.          It is my opinion that the STARS has become somewhat moribund
             of late and as the activities of the organisation are closely scrutinised,
             I consider that there is no need to feel that BI1Y security risk exists
             at present.

             6.          Any change in the siwatiion Rill , of· course, be watched.

                          A letter has beon received from the Department of Externn.l
               Affairs stating that the United States Embassy hris informally soueht
               their assistance in obtaining information which mieht bo used in the
               identification ol space vehicle fragments. The Embass7 stated that
               if a luminous object is observed in the atmo!3pl1ero and/ or fra[;"1011ts
               are recovered which might be associated with the re-entry or earth
               irnpaat· of space vehicles, the following lnformation wottl.d be helpful
               in identifying the vehicle and determining its origin :-
       •
               1. Obseryration of Luminous Objects
                    (a)   Date and time of the observation(e).
                    (b)   Location of the observer(s) {city, town, etc. or
                          geographic coordinates).
                    (c)   Trajectory or flieht path of the object (compass
                          heading) and its location relative to observer(a).
                    (d)   Burning characteristics, i.e. colour J 't.rhether light
                          was steady or pulantin~, whether streamers were
                          emitted {sparkler effect).                            f
                            •

                    (e)   Was break-up of the object observed?   If so, the
                          estimated geographic aoor~inates, locating tho
                          point of break-up.
                    (r)   Was noise associated with the flieht?
                2. r Qbservation of Imoact(o) and/or necovecy of Fragment(s)
                    (a)   Date and time of impact(s) or recovery or fra~t{e).
                    (b)   Location of observer(s) and/or coordinates locating
                          area where fragmsnts were recovered. ·
                    (c)   Dispersion of fragments within the recovery area.
                    (d)   Was noise associated with the impacts?
                                                                                                         •
                    (e)   If impacts were not observed, but heard, the data
                          and time of noise.
                    (£)   Physical description of the fraernonts, i.e. length,
                          width, thickness, weight, condition, etc.
                    (g)   Description of any numbers, letters, inspection                       ••
•                         stamps, or other identifying marks on the fragments •

                     extreme south of York Peninsular who witnessed what could well have been space
                     vehicle fragments on re-entry . Hereunder a.re relevant extracts from her report .
                              "     About 5.3opm I was returning home from football with my two girls
                                  then 11 and 6 years when about 20 yards from our garage I leaned
                                  forward to change gears and became aware of something falling from
                                  high up in the sky . It was dusk with a glow about the western and
                                  southern horizons.
                                    My first impression was that it looked like a large falling meteor,
                                  it seemed to be l-1reathed in a. smoky haze tbi s ma.de it look roundi ah
                                  there was some part of it alight though I could see no flames , the
                                  part alight seemed to be central . The direction of its fall seemed
                                  to be Sti' to NEs but more west than south. It was not fallin~
                                  straight down but at an angle a sharp angle (see illustration) I
                                  could not pick out any definite shape because of the smoky haze
                                  except that it appeared like a. round boulder encased in smoke. The
                                  smoke seemed to be going out,wards and backwards over it as it
                                  descended.
                                     The diffused light was still there. I stopped the car, switched
                                  off the dim lights, told the child1·en to wir1d. up the windows and lie
                                  on the floor of the car and I knelt down beside them, and saw that
                                  "it" wa.a much lower in the sky, it now was fully alight, with great
                                  tongues of flame pouring from the front part to the rear to encompass
                                  the whole thing, it not1 resembled in shape what I should imagine a
                                  wingless plane v1ould look like. The sheets of flame were ptllled
                                  backwards and appeared to be six to eight ft in length there was no
                                  trail at all apparent - no separating parts - it seemed to be all
                                  intact but \tell aliglit. The angle was not as acute the object
                                  appeared to be settling more into an ar~more west to east than
                                  S\v to NE. I'he a.re was alight more ~
                                                 1




                                     QuiC"..k1y the flames changed so the.t t11e obtlect looked like a fiery
                                  11ot ingot which gradually we11t blacl<: from the front por·tion to the
                                  rear. I can remember the ·f i11al glow as it lfas dying out ·then all
                                  was black and I saw 11othing for about two seconds . It wasnt very
                                  far fro1n gro1lnd level nol·T as I leaned against the windscreen ready
                                  at the last second to tl1row myself over the chj ldren in case of
                                  accident . And then I had my first clear and distinct picture of the
                                  shape and outline of what looked like a rocket, it 1vas much smaller
                                  than I anticipated probably 3 ft in length it            ,.,a.s
                                                                                           pointed directly
                                  to the east from the west flying parallel to the growid about 20 ft
                                  above the ground the front end was shaped like a. cone and tlie rest

                                  glow· f:ro1n tl1e south. It wa.s moving s1noothly but not a.t excessive

                           Next morning I tl1oroughly searched on area directly eas» of
                  the house. Just beyond the grove of trees the ground is fairly stoney,
                  wi tJ1 rock heaps and sheet rock. I looked for twisted wreckage at first
                  then I noticed very minute black pieces as tho something l1a.d shattered,
                  these were spread a.round fairly scattered. These bits were black they
                  resembled burnt paper but seemed to have more density than I imagined
                  burnt paper would have. I continued searching in widening circles and
                  eventually foWld in a ploughed up paddock due south of the scattered
                  black pieces about 250 yds awas - a small heap of blackened ma;terial.
                  The paddock had been ploughed up the ~ek previous ao the material was
                  undisturbed it rested in the furroas not particula.rJ.y imbedded if at
                  all. It resembled in appearances what loosely rolled bunit paper would
                  look like tho denser. It was about 1 2 to 15 ins high by 18 inches in
                  length. It was very black, I could see no printing as is usual \iith
                  burnt newspapers etc. Not caring to risk radio activity I found a
                  stick and prodded it, it broke up but not as lightly as paper wo1,tld.
                          I enquired fr<>ln my husband but definitely he had not burnt
                  a.nythihg there or furtl1er t.J.p where the black bits were. The paddock

                          I think I have it all dotm and ca.n add nothing further except

                  long branches there were gaps of course, it was only about 15 ft aw83 that I

              SUR]E<:T
                                                ALLEGED U.F.O. SIGHTING AT WOOMER!




                                                                                                                        •
                                          The queries raised in your memorandum of 2)rd June
                              (folio 17) and your footnote to teletype M6033 of 18th July from
                              Head Office (folio 19) have been investigated and reported upon by
                              PO/Ranges. I did not press for an immediate reply because -
                                    (a)         there was no note of urgency in your request;
                                    (b)         the matter (lid not come within tl\e normal
                                                duties of Ranges staff;
                                    (c)         the enquiry would obviously fall upon pro-
                                                fessional staff who are already overtaxed;
                                    (d)         all Ranges Group personnel have been more
                                                than fully extended in the conduct of SPARTA
                                                trials (continuously since early June) and
                                                the EUROPA F6/1 launching (since ear'  July).

                              2.          There are reports covering two separate alleged sightings,
                              namely the one on 27th April about which you enquired and another on
                              30th June. The latter is mentioned at the end of PO/R'a memorandum
                              of 17th July (folio 20A) to which are attached observers' reports
                              from several Range poets.

                              3.          The incident of 27th April is described generally in the
                              body of PO/R's memorandum of 17th July (folio 20A) and more specifically
                              in his subsequent memorandum of 21et July (folio 22) to which observers'
                              reports are attached. These reports, copies of which are held by
                              Head Office, are extraordinarily vague and incomplete and do not provide
                              a satisfactory basis for assessing the significance, if 8Jl3, of the
                              alleged "sighting". Kine operators are, after all, not trained
                              observers and do not necessarily have any scientific background.

                              4.          In these circ11mstancea, it is rather disturbing to find that
                              the Sighting Report Forms used on 27th April a.re headed "Scientific,
                              Technical and Astronomical Research Society". I have never heard of
                              such a society anywhere in Australia and certainly not at Woomera.

                              5.           In fact, I was quite unaware that a U.F.O. Society had been
                              established at Woomera. The last I heard of this proposal was your
                              memorandum of 11th May, 1966 to Superintendent/ Woomera saying that the
                              proposal could not be officially supported (folio 3 on File SA5753/1/2)o
                              I n fact, the tone of your memorandum clearly implied that the suggestion
                              should be discouraged.

                              6.          If Superintendent/Woomera agreed to the formation of the
                              Society I consider that you and I should have been advised of it. Not
                              only does the Society appear to exist but there is an arrangement
                              whereby reports (including reports of observations made on the Range,
                              using Range instrumentation) are passed to the Establishment Security
                              Officer, Woomera. Like you, I was completely unaware of this arrange-



                                                                                                                   /2


National Archives of Australia                                                            NAA: 0174, SA5644/2/1
                                               2.                         SA5644/2/1
                                                                          SA5753/1/2
                ment the purpose of which escapes me. The ESO Woomera, for reasons
                best known to himself, sent the April 27th forms to c.s.o. Melbourne
                with the result that we now find ourselves in a time-wasting and
                profitless exerciseo

                7.          I can see no virtue but only trouble in allowing an
                unscientific body such as a U.F.O. Society to function in the
                restricted area of Woomera especially if its members are allowed
                to use Range instrumentation in the course of their activities.
                There is a serious risk of an incident which could bring the
                Establishment into disrepute.

                B.          I would therefore recommend that, if permission has been
                given by the former Superintendent/Woomera for the formation of a
                U.F.O. Society in Woomera, it should now be withdrawn. In support
                of the recommendation it might be pointed out that ,_
                      (a)   Woomera is a restricted area in which
                            classified objects are launched into the
                            atmosphere;
                      (b)   "Sightill8a" by residents JD83 well be
                            associated with trials which, in many
                            cases, cannot be reported in public;
                      (c)   "Sightings", even in Woomera, will
                            tend to be made by people who have little
                            or no scientific knowledge or background
                            in such relevant fields as atmospheric
                            physics, astronomy, optical systems
                            (including false optical images) aero-
                            dynamics, etco
                      (d)   Reports by such people are of little value
                            unless assessed by trained scientific
                            observers and, even then, the result is
                            rarely worth the effort because of the
                            inadequacy of most of the reports.
                      (e)   Professional staff on the Woomera Range
                            do not have the time to waste on such
                            unproductive activities; additional
                            staff would have to be provided if oon-
                            sidered reports on all "sightinge't were
                            required.

                         SU B]El :T                 AIJ:.EGED U.F.O. SIGHTING.


                                                 In response to your memorandum SA. 5644/2/1 T. 5565/9/1 t
                                       dated 30th June, the following report is subnitted. At the outset
                                       may I make the point that prior to the receipt of your memorandum,
                                       I was unaware of' the alleged sighting.

                                       2.         From discussions with SO/OI, OIC/SOS and OIC/Mus, it is
                                       evident that three or four such nsightings" occur each year, usually
                                       in the late autumn months. The "objectstt a.re almost certainly \1ind-
                                       bo:rne spider v1ebs which are common at this time of year, being the
                                       normal method of dispersion of young spiders.

                                   r   3.        The fact that attempts to photograph these .. objects" have
                                       always been unsuccessful , tends to support the theory that they are
                                       in fact extremely tenuous bodies at no great distance from the observers .
                                       Under these circumstances, the high powered lenses used on Contraves
                                       Kinetheodolites, for example, would not focus the objects sharply, and
                                       the contrast of the resulting extra-focal image would be so low as not
                                       to register on the film .

                                       4.        Because of the lack of any documentary evidence that sightings
                                       are other tha n spider webs, it has never been felt necessary to take
                                       further action, and the information has remained with the Optical
                                       Instrumentation ~ection.

                                       5.        Where this present case differs from earlier sightings is in
                                       that it attracted the attention of a "U. F. O. Society" in the Village ,
                                       several of whose members work in Ranges Group. A condition of the
                                       formation of this society was that, any sightings made within the
                                       Woomera Area shou.ld be referred to Security Section . This was done and
                                       the affair was thence referred through normal Security channels to the
                                       Director. I have requested the Security Branch Off icers at the Range
                                       to consult me before forwarding such reports in the future. Unfortunately
                                       this was not done on the occasion under notice.

                                       6.        In future all such occurrences will be reported to your office ,
                                       as requested in your memorandum if you so desire . However I would suggest
                                       that only those sightings which can be substantiated by filmed evidence
                                       or those where there is a degree of correlation should be brought to your
                                       notice. In the meantime I have to report that "unidentified objects"
                           \
                                       were seen by several optical instrumentation operators just prior to the
                     ~ (J\             firing of a "Sea Dart'' round on 30th June . Instruments capable of
                                       ph0tographing without affecting their trials readiness were requested to
         .~""                  \
                                       do so, with the usual negative result.

                                       7.        The operators concerned with this latest "sight-.;i~" have com-
                                       pleted the standard Security 0 Report on Aerial Ob 'ect       ed'' , which

            *       Since it is normal ly impossible to estimate the height and speed of a

                    strange object it will usually be better to endeavour to det ermine the

                    angl e of elevati on of t he object, the angle through wit h it moved and
                    t he time taken t o do this .

           *      Since it is normally impossible to estimate the height and speed of a

                  strange object it will usually be better to endeavour to det ermine t he

                  angle of elevation of the object, the angle through winh it moved and
